is the initial exponential rate,
z_{1}
from condition (
5.52
).

Now, the continuity of
E_{s}^{n}(z,t)
with respect to time
t
ensures that a strict inequality holds, at least, in a slightly larger time interval
[0,t_{n}+\varepsilon)
,
\varepsilon>0
. Yet, this fact contradicts the maximality of
t_{n}
unless
t_{n}=+\infty
. Therefore,

E_{s}^{n}(z,t)<M_{P}+(e-1)\,m_{0}[f_{0}]
for all
t\geq 0
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Thus, letting
n\rightarrow\infty
we conclude

\displaystyle E_{s}^{n}[f](z,t)

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}E_{s}^{n}[f](z,t)\leq(e-1)m_{0}[f_{0}]+M_{P}

\displaystyle\qquad<(e-1)m_{0}[f_{0}]+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f_{0}(v)\,e^{z_{0}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{2s}}dv=(e-1)m_{0}[f_{0}]+E_{s}[f](z_{0},0),\quad\forall t\geq 0,

i.e. the solution
f(v,t)
to Boltzmann equation with finite initial exponential moment of order
2s
and rate
z_{0}
propagates the exponential moments of order
2s
, but at a lower constant rate
z
than the initial rate
z_{0}
, with
z
defined by

z(t):=z<\min\left\{z_{0},\,\ln e,\,z_{1}\right\}=\left\{z_{0},\,1,\,z_{1}\right\},

(5.55)

for
z_{1}
defined by (
5.52
), and well characterized by (
5.53
).

∎

Proof of Theorem
25
.

(
Generation of Exponential moments
)
This proof, has similarities with respect to the one of previous Theorem
24
, mostly in the sense that we seek an ODI for the exponential moment, denoted for simplicity of notation, by
E_{s}[f](t):=E_{z,s}[f](t)
where the exponential order factor
2s
and rate
z(t)
need to be found and fully characterized, by
the initial data
f_{in}\in L^{1}_{k_{in}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for
k_{in}\geq 2\epsilon+1
, by the potential rate
\gamma\in(0,2]
and and by properties on the binary collision operator as described for Cauchy Problem stated in Theorem
7
. That means the initial data is defined takes the form
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f_{0}(v)\,\left\langle v\right\rangle^{k_{in}}\mathrm{d}v=:M_{G}
finite, with
k_{in}\geq{\bf k_{c}}>1
, as defined in (
5.7
).

We require
z(t)
to be continuous, increasing for
t\in(0,1]
and with
z(0)=0
since the exponential weight is not available at
t=0
.

As the proof for propagation of exponential tails was initiated, in Theorem
24
, recall the first the
sk^{th}
-moments generation global bounds derived in Theorem
17
, (
3.7
), which can be large for short times.

Set constant
C_{\gamma k_{0}}>0
defined by the upper bound of first
sk_{0}
finite moments, and invoke the
sk
-parameters introduced in the polynomial moments estimates, to obtain, for any
0<s\leq 1<s{\bf k_{c}}<sk.

Then, starting from the solution of the Boltzmann flow for
f_{in}(v)
, constructed in Theorem
7
, with the solution set
\Omega
as defined in (
4.4
),
our task consists on studying the time evolution of generated moment partial power series in
z(t)
up to order
n
, denoted
E^{n}_{s}(z(t),t)
, with the goal to find uniform in
n
rates
z(t)>0
dependent by the given initial data.

As performed in the proof of the propagation of exponential tail estimates, the corresponding moments ODI satisfied by the solution
f
of the Cauchy problem posed in Theorem
7
written as in (
5.12
) becomes again the starting point and the representation of
I^{n}_{s,\gamma}[f]
defined explicitly in (
5.56
), is now invoked evaluated at
(z(t),t)
to obtain

I^{n}_{s,\gamma}[f](z(t),t):=\sum_{k=0}^{n}m_{sk+\frac{\gamma}{2}}[f](t)\frac{z^{k}(t)}{k!}\,.

(5.56)

Thus, starting first for values of
t\in(0,1]
and invoking the product rule and the fact that contractive constant
\mu_{k}
decreasing in
k
,
I^{n}_{s,\gamma}(z(t),t)
given now by the
n^{th}
-partial sum of the shifted to the
sk+\gamma/2
-moment defined in (
5.56
), after some elementary algebra it follows that for
t\in(0,1]
.
Hence, taking the rate of change of the partial moment’s sums

E^{n}_{s}(z(t),t)
, with respect to time
t
, and invoking the
n^{th}
-partial
sk^{th}
-Lesbegue moments sums and the more accurate binary expansion for moments estimates from the collisional form (
26
), the for a suitable
k_{0}>{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}
to be chosen later, the following estimate holds

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E^{n}_{s}(z(t),t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}m^{\prime}_{sk}[f]\frac{z^{k}(t)}{k!}+\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{n}m^{\prime}_{sk}[f]\frac{z^{k}(t)}{k!}+z^{\prime}(t)\sum_{k=1}^{n}m_{sk}[f]\frac{z^{k-1}(t)}{(k-1)!}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}m^{\prime}_{sk}[f]\frac{z^{k}(t)}{k!}+\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{n}\frac{z^{k}(t)}{k!}\Big(2^{\frac{3\gamma}{2}-1}C_{\Phi}\mu_{sk}S_{sk}[f]-\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}m_{sk+\frac{\gamma}{2}}[f]\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 122.34692pt+z^{\prime}(t)\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}m_{sk+\frac{\gamma}{2}}[f]\,\frac{z^{k}(t)}{(k)!}

(5.57)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}\left(m^{\prime}_{sk}[f]+\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}m_{sk+\frac{\gamma}{2}}[f]\right)\frac{z^{k}(t)}{k!}+2^{\frac{3\gamma}{2}-1}C_{\Phi}\mu_{sk}\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{n}\frac{z^{k}(t)}{k!}S_{sk}[f]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}I^{n}_{s,\gamma}(z(t),t)+z^{\prime}(t)I^{n-1}_{s,\gamma}(z(t),t),

much like the split made in the Propagation of partial some case (
5.1
), and so the right hand side from (
5.1
) will be estimated for times adapting the estimates performed in (
5.1
), taking into account that the unknown exponetial rate
z=z(t)
depends on time
t\in(0,1]
.

Hence, invoking the global moments generation estimates from (
3.7
), for
c_{\gamma}(sk)=\frac{\gamma}{2(sk-1)}
, for any
sk\geq s{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}>2
,
the first step starting from consists in performing the
m^{\prime}_{sk}[f]
estimates similar to those on (
5.34
), for any
0<sk\leq s{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}
but now applying the generation upper bounds (
5
) when needed.
In addition, if the initial data
M_{G}=m_{s{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}}[f_{1}](0)
, and
sk_{0}\geq s{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}
, then

\displaystyle m_{sk}^{\prime}[f](t)

\displaystyle<\!{\tt\bf m}_{1,sk}[f_{0}]\,\beta_{sk}\,R_{{\mathcal{C}}}(\delta^{-1}_{sk})\mathbb{I}_{k\geq{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}+1}\

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\ 2^{\frac{\frac{{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}}{2}+3\gamma}{2}}{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}C_{\Phi}m_{1+\frac{\gamma}{2}}[f](t)m_{s{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}+\frac{\gamma}{2}}[f](t)\mathbb{I}_{k<{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}+1}

\displaystyle<{\tt\bf m}_{1,sk}[f_{0}]\,\beta_{sk}\,R_{{\mathcal{C}}}(\delta^{-1}_{sk})\,\mathbb{I}_{k\geq{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}+1}+\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}2^{\frac{\frac{{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}}{2}+3\gamma}{2}}\!{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}C_{\Phi}\left[\left(R_{\mathbb{E}}(\delta^{-1}_{s+\frac{\gamma}{2}})+\frac{m_{1}[f_{0}]}{\gamma\,A_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad\left(R_{\mathbb{E}}(\delta^{-1}_{s{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})+m_{1}[f_{0}]\max_{k<{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}+1}\left(\!\frac{1}{c_{\gamma}(sk\!+\!\frac{\gamma}{2})\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{c_{\gamma}(sk\!+\!\frac{\gamma}{2})}}\right)t^{-\frac{1}{c_{\gamma}(sk\!+\!\frac{\gamma}{2})}}\right]\,\mathbb{I}_{k<{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}+1}

\displaystyle<\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\left(\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk})+{{\mathcal{C}}}^{G}_{\bar{k}}\,t^{-\frac{2sk\!+\!\gamma-2}{\gamma}}\right),

(5.58)

with the time constant factors

\displaystyle\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}:=\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d\!-\!1})}\left(C_{\Phi}\left(2^{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}}\!+\!2\!\right)+\!2^{\frac{\frac{{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}}{2}+3\gamma}{2}}\right)\,\max\left\{\left(m_{1};{\tt\bf m}_{1,sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}};\left(m_{1}\,{\tt\bf m}_{1,sk_{0}}\right)^{\!\frac{sk_{0}-1}{s{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mn}}}+\frac{\gamma}{2}}}\right)\right\}[f_{0}]

(5.59)

and

{{\mathcal{C}}}^{G}_{\bar{k}}
denoting, for any moment’s order
1<k\leq\bar{k}
such that
s\bar{k}\geq sk>1
and
0<s\leq 1
,

\displaystyle{\bf{\mathcal{C}}}^{G}_{\bar{k}}:=\max_{k<\bar{k}+1}\!\!\left(\!\frac{c^{-1}_{\gamma}(sk\!+\!\frac{\gamma}{2})}{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}\right)^{c^{-1}_{\gamma}(sk\!+\!\frac{\gamma}{2})}.

(5.60)

Since the positive coercive constant
\mathcal{A}_{\bf k_{c}}=c_{lb}2^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}{A_{\bf k_{c}}}
, may or may not be bigger that unity, and the power associated to the quotient
c^{-1}_{\gamma}(sk+\gamma/2)/\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}
needs to be maximize between
1<sk<s\bar{k}
, then
the corresponding constant upper
{\bf{\mathcal{C}}}^{G}_{k_{0}}
bound associated to (
5.60
) is simply stated to be

\displaystyle{\bf{\mathcal{C}}}^{G}_{k_{0}}:=\max_{k\leq k_{0}+1}\left(\!\frac{sk-1+\gamma/2}{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}\,\gamma/2}\right)^{\!\!\frac{sk-1+\gamma/2}{\gamma/2}}.

(5.61)

Therefore, for any
t\in(0,1]
and moment order
k_{0}\geq 0
, the first term in that last RHS of (
5
) estimates the partial moments sum majoring with the constant
{{\mathcal{C}}}^{G}_{k}
from (
5.60
) by
{{\mathcal{C}}}^{G}_{sk_{0}}
, and natural upper bounds for
m^{\prime}_{sk+\frac{\gamma}{2}}
from (
5.1
)
as follows

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}m_{sk+\frac{\gamma}{2}}[f]+m^{\prime}_{sk}[f]\right)\frac{z(t)^{k}}{k!}<\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}\left[{\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}m_{sk+\frac{\gamma}{2}}[f]+\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\left(\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk})+{{\mathcal{C}}}^{G}_{\bar{k}}\,t^{-\frac{2sk\!+\!\gamma-2}{\gamma}}\right)\right]\frac{z(t)^{k}}{k!}

\displaystyle\ \ <{\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}\left(\left(m_{1}\,{\tt\bf m}_{1,sk_{0}}\right)^{\!\frac{sk_{0}-1}{s{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mn}}}+\frac{\gamma}{2}}}\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})\max_{0<t\leq 1}e^{z(t)}+m_{1}[f_{0}]\,{{\mathcal{C}}}^{G}_{sk_{0}}\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}t^{-{\frac{sk-1+\gamma/2}{\gamma/2}}}\frac{z(t)^{k}}{k!}\!\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}})\max_{0<t\leq 1}e^{z(t)}+\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}{{\mathcal{C}}_{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}^{G}}\,\sum_{k=0}^{{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}+1}t^{-\frac{2sk+\gamma-2}{\gamma}}\frac{z(t)^{k}}{k!}

(5.62)

\displaystyle\quad<\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}+1\right)\max_{0<t\leq 1}e^{z(t)}\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})+\left[{\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}\,m_{1}[f_{0}]\,{\bf{\mathcal{C}}}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}}^{G}+\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\,{\mathcal{C}}_{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}^{G}\right]\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}\,t^{-\frac{2sk+\gamma-2}{\gamma}}\frac{z(t)^{k}}{k!}.

with the constant rate factor
\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}}):=\mathcal{R}(\beta_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}}/\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}})

is from (
5.28
), provided that
sk_{0}>1
.

It follows that estimate (
5.63
) can be expressed term of these constant factors (
5.59
) by

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}m_{sk+\frac{\gamma}{2}}[f]+m^{\prime}_{sk}[f]\right)\frac{z(t)^{k}}{k!}<\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}+1\right)\left(\max_{0<t\leq 1}e^{z(t)}\,\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})+{\bf{\mathcal{C}}}_{\text{\bf max}}^{G}\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}\,{t}^{-\frac{2sk_{0}+\gamma-2}{\gamma}}\frac{z(t)^{k}}{k!}\right),

(5.63)

for

\displaystyle{\bf{\mathcal{C}}}_{\text{\bf max}}^{G}=\max\left\{m_{1}[f_{0}]\,{\bf{\mathcal{C}}}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}}^{G}\,\,;\,{\mathcal{C}}_{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}^{G}\right\}

(5.64)

The second term from (
5.1
) is estimated by the Discrete Convolution Lemma
27
set up for
\tilde{k}=k_{0}
, inequality (
5.33
),
now for any
s\leq\gamma/2
,
yields

2^{\frac{3\gamma}{2}-1}C_{\Phi}\mu_{sk_{0}}\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{n}\frac{z^{k}(t)}{k!}S_{sk}[f]\leq 2^{\frac{3\gamma}{2}-1}C_{\Phi}\mu_{sk}\ E^{n}_{s}(z(t),t)\,I^{n}_{s,\gamma}(z(t),t),\quad\text{for any}\ 0<t\leq 1.

(5.65)

Then, the last term from the right hand side of (
5.1
) is gathered by the fact that
I^{n-1}_{s,\gamma}(z(t),t)\leq I^{n}_{s,\gamma}(z(t),t)
together with (
5.63
) and (
5.65
) to obtain upper bounds
to the ODI for
E^{n}_{s}(t,z\,t)
as follows

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E^{n}_{s}(t,z(t))

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}+1\right)\left(\max_{0<t\leq 1}e^{z(t)}\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})+{\bf{\mathcal{C}}}_{\text{\bf max}}^{G}\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}\,{t}^{-\frac{2sk_{0}+\gamma-2}{\gamma}}\frac{z(t)^{k}}{k!}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-I^{n}_{s,\gamma}(z(t),t)\left(\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}-z^{\prime}(t)-2^{\frac{3\gamma}{2}-1}C_{\Phi}\mu_{sk_{0}}\ E^{n}_{s}(z(t),t)\right)\quad\text{for any}\ \ 0<t\leq 1,

(5.66)

Further, the negative term proportional to the shifted moments partial sums
I^{n}_{s,\gamma}(z(t),t)
, is estimated from below by

\displaystyle{I}^{n}_{s;\gamma}(z(t),t)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\,\frac{z^{k}(t)}{k!}m_{sk+\gamma/2}(t)=\frac{1}{z(t)}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}(k+1)\,\frac{z^{k+1}(t)}{(k+1)!}m_{sk+\gamma/2}(t)\right)

(5.67)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{z(t)}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n+1}\,\frac{z^{k}(t)}{k!}m_{sk}(t)\right)>\frac{1}{z(t)}\left(E^{n}_{s}(z(t),t)-m_{0}[f_{0}]\right)\geq 0,

which implies, under conditions
0<t\leq 1
and
0<s\leq\gamma/2

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E^{n}_{s}(t,z(t))

\displaystyle<\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}+1\right)\left(\max_{0<t\leq 1}e^{z(t)}\,\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})+{\bf{\mathcal{C}}}_{\text{\bf max}}^{G}\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}\,{t}^{-\frac{2sk_{0}+\gamma-2}{\gamma}}\frac{z(t)^{k}}{k!}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\frac{(E^{n}_{s}(z(t),t)-m_{0}[f_{0}])}{z(t)}\left(\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}-z^{\prime}(t)-2^{\frac{3\gamma}{2}-1}C_{\Phi}\mu_{sk_{0}}\ E^{n}_{s}(z(t),t)\right).

(5.68)

Since the latter term in the right side needs to be an absorption term, we require the condition on the exponential rate
z(t)
that maximizes is the largest possible one satisfying the condition

\displaystyle z^{\prime}(t)\leq\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}\ \ \ \ \text{for any}\ 0<t<1.

(5.69)

In particular, after performing a time integration recalling that
z(0)=0
, the natural choice is
0\leq z(t):=zt\leq\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}t
, and therefore the
z
factor must satisfy

\displaystyle z\leq\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}

(5.70)

Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E^{n}_{s}(t,z(t))

\displaystyle<\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}+1\right)\left(\max_{0<t\leq 1}e^{z(t)}\,\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})+{\bf{\mathcal{C}}}_{\text{\bf max}}^{G}\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}\,{t}^{-\frac{2sk_{0}+\gamma-2}{\gamma}}\frac{z(t)^{k}}{k!}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{(E^{n}_{s}(z(t),t)-m_{0}[f_{0}])}{z(t)}\left(\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}-2^{\frac{3\gamma}{2}-1}C_{\Phi}\mu_{sk_{0}}\ E^{n}_{s}(z(t),t)\right).

Now, invoking (
5.70
) for the choice
z(t)=\frac{\mathcal{A}_{\bf k_{c}}}{2}t
, then for any
t\in(0,1]
, it follows

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}

\displaystyle\,{t}^{-\frac{1}{c_{\gamma}(sk)}}\frac{z(t)^{k}}{k!}=\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}t^{-\frac{2sk-\gamma+2}{\gamma}}t^{k}\frac{1}{k!}\left(\frac{\mathcal{A}_{\bf k_{c}}}{2}\right)^{k}=\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}t^{{k}(1-\frac{2s}{\gamma})+(1-\frac{2}{\gamma})}\frac{1}{k!}\left(\frac{\mathcal{A}_{\bf k_{c}}}{2}\right)^{k}<e^{\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}},

(5.71)

for any
s\in(0,\gamma/2]
and
\gamma\in(0,2]
.

To this end, a reduced ODI for the partial sums of
sk^{th}
-moments arises

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E^{n}_{s}(t,zt)

\displaystyle<\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}+1\right)e^{\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}}\left(\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})+{\bf{\mathcal{C}}}_{\text{\bf max}}^{G}\right)

(5.72)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{(E^{n}_{s}(t,zt)-m_{0}[f_{0}])}{zt}\left(\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}-2^{\frac{3\gamma}{2}-1}C_{\Phi}\mu_{sk_{0}}\ E^{n}_{s}(z(t),t)\right),\ \ \text{for any }\ 0<t<1.

Next, recall that

m_{0}[f_{0}]<\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f_{0}(v)\langle v\rangle^{2k_{in}}dv=:M_{G},\quad\text{for}\ \ n\in\mathbb{N}\,.

(5.73)

Therefore, since
m_{0}[f_{0}](0)=E^{n}_{s}(0,z(0))<M_{G}
, proceeding in an analog way that we did for the exponential propagation, we set the following sequence of times
t_{n}
, motivated by the search of the exponential rate
z
small enough that enables an upper bound for
E^{n}_{s}(t,zt)
, given by

t_{n}:=\sup\big\{t\in(0,1]\,:\,E^{n}_{s}(\tau,z(\tau))\leq M_{G},\;\forall\,\tau\in[0,t]\big\}\,,\quad\text{for}\ \ \ n\in\mathbb{N},

(5.74)

where
t_{n}\leq 1
is the first touching time where
E^{n}_{s}(t,z(t))=M_{G}
, with

M_{G}
is the initial moment with
k_{in}^{th}
-order Lebesgue weighted tail.

In addition invoking moment generation estimates up to
k=n
, for any time
0<t<1
, it holds that,

t^{k}\,m_{sk}(t)\leq t^{k}\,\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})+{\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}^{-\frac{2sk-\gamma+2}{\gamma}}\,t^{k(1-\frac{2}{\gamma}s)+\frac{2}{\gamma}}\,,\quad\text{for}\quad 1\leq k\leq n\,,

so that, if
s\in(0,\frac{\gamma}{2}]
and
\gamma\in(0,2]
, the limit as
t\searrow 0
of this product vanishes. Hence,

\displaystyle E^{n}_{s}(t,z(t))

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\,\frac{(zt)^{k}}{k!}m_{sk}(t)=m_{0}[f_{0}]+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\,\frac{(zt)^{k}}{k!}m_{sk}(t)\leq m_{0}[f_{0}]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\left(e^{\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}}-1\right)\left(\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})+{\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}^{-\frac{2sk-\gamma+2}{\gamma}}\right)\,t^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}\,.

(5.75)

Consequently, since
m_{0}<M_{G}
, it holds that

E^{n}_{s}(t,z(t))\leq M_{G}\quad\text{in the interval}\quad 0<t\leq\left(\frac{{\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}^{\frac{2sk-\gamma+2}{\gamma}}\left(M_{G}-m_{0}[f_{0}]\right)}{\left(e^{\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}}-1\right)\,\left(\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}}){\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}^{\frac{2sk-\gamma+2}{\gamma}}+1\right)}\right)^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\ =:\ T_{k_{0}}\,.

This guarantees that the differential estimate (
5.72
) satisfied by
E^{n}_{s}(t,z(t))
holds in the non-empty interval
t\in(0,t_{n}]
where

1\geq t_{n}\geq T_{k_{0}}\,,\quad\text{for all}\quad n\in\mathbb{N}\,,

or equivalently, the set (
5.74
) is non-empty.

In addition, due to the fact that the contractive factor
0<\mu_{sk_{0}}\searrow 0
, with a sufficiently large
k_{0}:=k_{0}({\bf k_{c}},M_{G})
, renders the lower bound estimate

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}-2^{\frac{3\gamma}{2}-1}C_{\Phi}\mu_{sk_{0}}\ E^{n}_{s}(t,zt)\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}-2^{\frac{3\gamma}{2}-1}\,C_{\Phi}\,\mu_{sk_{0}}\,M_{G}\geq\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{4},

to hold for the precise, uniform in
n\in\mathbb{N}
, choice of

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mu_{s(k_{0}-1)}\leq\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2^{\frac{3\gamma+2}{2}}C_{\Phi}M_{G}},\qquad\text{for any}\quad 0<s\leq\frac{\gamma}{2}\quad\text{and}\quad 0<t\leq t_{n}\leq 1.

(5.76)

In particular,
Remark
19
stated that for any the angular transition
b(\hat{u}\cdot\sigma)\in L^{p}(S^{d-1})
, for
1<p\leq\infty
, invoking (
3.31
) or (
3.32
), the
k_{0}^{th}
-moment becomes explicit

\displaystyle sk_{0}>\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{2^{\frac{3\gamma+4}{2}}C_{\Phi}M_{G}}{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}\right)^{\frac{p}{p-1}},\qquad\text{for}\ k_{0}>{\bf k_{c}}\ \text{and}\ 0<s\leq 1.

(5.77)

Therefore, directly from estimate (
5.72
), substituting
z(t)=zt
it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E^{n}_{s}(t,zt)

\displaystyle<\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}+1\right)e^{\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}}\left(\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})+{\bf{\mathcal{C}}}_{\text{\bf max}}^{G}\right)-\frac{\mathcal{A}_{{\bf k_{c}}}}{4zt}\big(E^{n}_{s}(t,zt)-m_{0}[f_{0}]\big)

(5.78)

\displaystyle\text{valid for}\quad 0<t\leq t_{n}\leq 1\quad\text{and}\quad s\in\left(0,\frac{\gamma}{2}\right]\,.

where the coefficients of this new ODI are uniform in
n
.

Hence, in order to find bound to solutions of the initial value problem to inequality (
5.78
), it is convenient to set
X^{n}_{k_{0}}(t,zt):=E_{s}^{n}(t,zt)-m_{0}[f_{0}]\geq 0
, to obtain the contractive ODI

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}X^{n}(t,zt)<\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}+1\right)e^{\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}}\left(\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})+{\bf{\mathcal{C}}}_{\text{\bf max}}^{G}\right)-\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{4zt}\,X^{n}(t,zt)\,,\qquad 0<t\leq t_{n}\leq 1\,,

\displaystyle X^{n}(0,0):=\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}E_{s}^{n}(t,zt)-m_{0}[f_{0}]=0\qquad\text{its inital data}.

(5.79)

A function satisfying the differential inequality (
5.1
) is controlled by direct integration of the inequality
\bar{X}^{\prime}+c\,t^{-1}\bar{X}<K
after using the multiplicative factor
t^{c}
so that
(t^{c}\bar{X})^{\prime}\leq t^{c}\,K
, for
t>0
. We arrive to

\bar{X}<\bar{X}(0)+t(1+c)^{-1}B\,,\quad t>0\,.

When this super solution is applied with
c:=\frac{\mathcal{A}_{{\bf k_{c}}}}{2z}
and
K:=\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}+1\right)e^{\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}}\left(\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})+{\bf{\mathcal{C}}}_{\text{\bf max}}^{G}\right)
, and initial
\bar{X}(0)=0
the target function
X^{n}(t,zt)
is then controlled by

\displaystyle X^{n}(t,zt)

\displaystyle<\frac{4zt}{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}+4z}\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}+1\right)e^{\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}}\left(\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})+{\bf{\mathcal{C}}}_{\text{\bf max}}^{G}\right)

(5.80)

\displaystyle\ \ \ <\frac{4zt}{\mathcal{A}_{{\bf k_{c}}}}\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}+1\right)e^{\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}}\left(\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})+{\bf{\mathcal{C}}}_{\text{\bf max}}^{G}\right)\qquad\text{for any}\ 0<t\leq t_{n}\leq 1.

Therefore choosing
zt>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{4zt}{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}+1\right)e^{\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}}\left(\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})+{\bf{\mathcal{C}}}_{\text{\bf max}}^{G}\right)<M_{G}-m_{0}[f_{0}],

or equivalently,

(5.81)

\displaystyle{0<zt}<\frac{(M_{G}-m_{0}[f_{0}])\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}e^{-\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}}}{4\,\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}+1\right)\left(\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})+{\bf{\mathcal{C}}}_{\text{\bf max}}^{G}\right)},\qquad\text{for any}\ 0<t\leq t_{n}\leq 1,

it follows that, for any
0<t\leq t_{n}\leq 1
,

\displaystyle E_{s}^{n}(t,zt)-m_{0}[f_{0}]

\displaystyle<\frac{4zt}{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}+1\right)e^{\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}}\left(\mathcal{R}(\delta^{-1}_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})+{\bf{\mathcal{C}}}_{\text{\bf max}}^{G}\right)<M_{G}-m_{0}[f_{0}],

implying

\displaystyle E_{s}^{n}(t,zt)<M_{G},\qquad\text{for any}\ 0<t\leq t_{n}\leq 1.

(5.82)

Since
t\leq 1
we conclude that any constant parameter
z
, uniformly in
n\in\mathbb{N}
, satisfying

\displaystyle 0<z<\min\left\{\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}\ ;\ \mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}\frac{\mathcal{R}(\delta_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})(M_{G}-m_{0}[f_{0}])\,e^{-\frac{\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}}{2}}}{4\kappa^{G}_{k_{0}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}+1\right)\left(1+\mathcal{R}(\delta_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}}){\bf{\mathcal{C}}}_{\text{\bf max}}^{G}\right)}\right\},

(5.83)

is the admissible rate, such that
E^{n}_{s}(t,zt)<M_{G}
for
0<t\leq t_{n}\leq 1
, for any
s\in(0,\gamma/2]
and the constant
\kappa^{g}_{sk_{0}}
defined in (
5.59
).

Since the rate factor
0<{\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{A}_{s{\bf k_{c}}}/\beta_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})=\mathcal{R}(\delta_{sk_{0}+\frac{\gamma}{2}})<1
, the exponential rate
z
will not degenerate for any
0<\gamma\leq 2
. In addition, recalling
\mathcal{A}_{{\bf k_{c}}}:=c_{lb}2^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}A_{s{\bf k_{c}}}
, the form of the rate of decay for the exponential tail, depending on the coercive factor
A_{s{\bf k_{c}}}
, by replacing on (
5.83
), to obtain the rate (
5.6
) in the statement of generation of exponential moments.

Note that the inequality (
5.82
) for the choice of such rate
z(t)=zt
is strict, that means there is no touching time in
(0,t_{n}]
, so it must be the case that
t_{n}=1
due to time continuity of the moments evolution.

Sending
n\rightarrow\infty
one concludes

\displaystyle E_{s}(t,zt)<M_{G},\quad\ \text{for all}\quad 0\leq t\leq 1,\quad s\in\left(0,\frac{\gamma}{2}\right]\,.

(5.84)

Note that the dependence of
z
is in terms of the conserved quantities, that is, the mass and energy of
f
and on
M^{\star}_{G}
, defined as
M^{\star}_{G}:=\sup_{t\geq 0}m_{k_{in}}[f(t)]
, replacing
M_{G}
. All such quantities are independently on time. Therefore, by the time-invariance of the equation, one redoes the argument in the intervals
t\in[{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}/2,1+{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}/2]
, with
{\bf{k}_{\text{\tiny mx}}}=1,2,\cdots
, to conclude that

\displaystyle E_{s}(t,zt)\leq M^{*}_{G},\quad\ \text{for all}\quad t\geq 0,\quad s\in\left(0,\frac{\gamma}{2}\right]\,.

(5.85)

The proof Theorem
25
is now completed.

∎

6.
Convolution inequalities and gain of integrability for the collision operator

After the groundbreaking work of Arkeryd
[
20
]
in 1982 on propagation of
L^{\infty}
estimates for bounded angular cross-sections, in late eighties and nineties Gustaffson
[
62
]
, Wennberg
[
91
]
, Lions
[
69
]
, among others, studied
L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
estimates, for
1\leq r<\infty
, for the collision operator. Lions introduced the concept of
gain of integrabilty
, by meaning that it is possible to obtain smoothing properties of the collision operator under certain regularity conditions of the potential kernel. His work culminated in estimates of the form

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q^{+}(f,g)(v,t)f(v,t)dv\leq C(\|g\|_{L^{1}})\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{2-\delta},\quad\text{for some}\quad\delta\in(0,1)\,.

Yet, both constants
C(\|g\|_{L^{1}})
and
\delta\in(0,1)
where shown to exist but hard to calculate. These results provided a venue to obtain energy estimates suitable for the study of the evolution of
L^{r}
-norms for solutions to the Boltzmann equation later in
[
89
,
75
]
. Such initial results where limited to potentials satisfying certain regularity conditions in both kinetic and angular variables, including angular cut-off near
\hat{u}\cdot\sigma=\pm 1
.

The new approach presented here comprises recent versions of functional inequalities that will enable to address the study of the propagation for the
L^{r}
-norms for the Boltzmann equation, not only under general integrable angular kernels for the first time, but also following estimates based in previous work
[
5
,
6
,
12
]
, it is possible to obtain explicit constants important to describe the dependence of the key parameters at play such as the angular transition function.

Motivated by this goal, we revisit the Young’s inequality’s proof with techniques introduced by the authors and Carneiro in
[
5
,
6
]
, based on radial symmetrization techniques applied to the weak form of the gain collision Boltzmann operator, not by invoking the classical representation (
3.5
,
3.6
), but rather written in terms of center of mass and relative velocity coordinates as described in (
2.3
) taking the form
v^{\prime}_{*}-v=-u^{+}:=-\tfrac{1}{2}\big(u+|u|\sigma\big)
and
v^{\prime}-v=u^{-}:=\tfrac{1}{2}(-u+|u|\sigma)
, as follows.

6.1.
Young’s type inequality for the gain collision operator

In the late eighties, it was show in
[
62
]
that the gain part of the collision operator satisfies Young’s convolution inequalities. Indeed, as the reader may have noticed, the gain collision operator is a special type of nonlinear convolution between the entries and the collision kernel. The reader can find in
[
5
,
6
]
the following version of such convolution inequalities. These estimates will be important for the propagation of
L^{r}
-norms, for the range
1<r\leq\infty
, to be shown as an application to the Boltzmann equation in the next section.

Theorem 28

(
Young’s type inequality).

Let
1\leq p,\,q,\,r\leq\infty
with
1/p+1/q=1+1/r
. Assume that

B(|u|,\hat{u}\cdot\omega)=b(\hat{u}\cdot\omega)\in L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})\,.

Then, the bilinear operator
Q^{+}
satisfies

\left\|Q^{+}(f,g)\right\|_{L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C_{b}(p,q)\,\|f\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,\|g\|_{L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

(6.1)

The constant
C_{b}(p,q)
is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{b}(p,q)=&\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left(\tfrac{1+e_{1}\cdot\sigma}{2}\right)^{-\tfrac{d}{2r^{\prime}}}b(e_{1}\cdot\sigma)d\sigma\right)^{\tfrac{r^{\prime}}{p^{\prime}}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left(\tfrac{1-e_{1}\cdot\sigma}{2}\right)^{-\tfrac{d}{2r^{\prime}}}b(e_{1}\cdot\sigma)d\sigma\right)^{\tfrac{r^{\prime}}{q^{\prime}}}.\end{split}

(6.2)

In the case
(p,q,r)=(1,1,1)
the constant
C_{b}(1,1)
is understood as

C_{b}(1,1)=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}b(e_{1}\cdot\sigma)d\sigma=\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}.

(6.3)

In the cases that
p=1
or
q=1
one interprets
(\cdot)^{r^{\prime}/p^{\prime}}=(\cdot)^{0}=1
and
(\cdot)^{r^{\prime}/q^{\prime}}=(\cdot)^{0}=1
respectively.

It is of interest to recall that Young’s inequality framework’s proof that developed by introducing
a weighted
\mathcal{P}
-operator as a function of the the angular integration, written in center of mass and relative velocity coordinates (
2.3
) is defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}({\it\Psi},{\it\Phi})(u)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}{\it\Psi}(u^{-}(u,\sigma){\it\Phi}(u^{+}(u,\sigma))b(\hat{u}\cdot\sigma)d\sigma,\ \qquad u^{\pm}:=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(u\pm|u|\sigma\big)\,

(6.4)

Indeed, the operator
\mathcal{P}
in (
6.4
) used in the weak formulation plays the central role of angular averaging occurring in
Q^{+}(f,f)(v)
as will be explained below, after Theorem
29
.
From this representation, it is clear that the integrability properties of
\mathcal{P}
are central to the understanding of the integrability properties of
Q^{+}
itself.
More precisely, using radial symmetrization one can readily prove the following result,

Theorem 29
.

Let
1\leq p,q,r\leq\infty
with
1/p+1/q=1/r
. Then,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}_{b}({\it\Psi},{\it\Phi})\|_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq\tilde{C}_{b}(p,q)\|{\it\Psi}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|\it\Phi\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

where

(6.5)

\displaystyle\tilde{C}_{b}(p,q)=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left(\tfrac{1-e_{1}\cdot\sigma}{2}\right)^{-\frac{d}{2p^{\prime}}}\left(\tfrac{1+e_{1}\cdot\sigma}{2}\right)^{-\frac{d}{2q^{\prime}}}\,b\big(e_{1}\cdot\sigma\big)d\sigma.

In addition, if either functions
\it\Psi
or
\it\Phi
are unity, but not simultaneously, then the statement (
29
) is modified by setting
q=\infty,

r=p
, since
1=\phi\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
to estimate, for
\mathbb{S}_{\mp}^{d-1}:=\{e_{1}\cdot\sigma\lessgtr 0\},

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b}({\it\Psi},1)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}:=\|\mathcal{P}_{b}({\it\Psi},1)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}_{b}(p,\infty)\|\it\Psi\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

\displaystyle\ \text{with}\ \tilde{C}_{b}(p,\infty)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{S}_{-}^{d-1}}\left(\tfrac{1-e_{1}\cdot\sigma}{2}\right)^{-\frac{d}{2p^{\prime}}}b\big(e_{1}\cdot\sigma\big)d\sigma,

\displaystyle\text{or setting}\ p=\infty,r=q,\ \text{for}\ 1=\psi\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1}),

to estimate

(6.6)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b}(1,{\it\Phi})\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}:=\|\mathcal{P}_{b}(1,{\it\Phi})\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}_{b}(\infty,q)\|\it\Phi\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

\displaystyle\text{with}\ \ \tilde{C}_{b}(\infty,q)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{S}_{+}^{d-1}}\left(\tfrac{1+e_{1}\cdot\sigma}{2}\right)^{-\frac{d}{2q^{\prime}}}b\big(e_{1}\cdot\sigma\big)d\sigma.

All details on how to proof Therorem
29
can be found at
[
5
, Theorem 1]
, as well as at
[
6
]
for further applications to Young’s inequalities for gain operators.
Theorem
29
is the base to prove Theorem
28
. It also explains the peculiar expression of the constants in such theorem. Interestingly, the constants given in Theorem
29
are known to be sharp in the sense of functional inequalities.

However, it is important to notice that the definition of
\mathcal{P}_{b}(\psi,\phi)(u)
in (
29
) depends on test functions
\psi
and
\phi
acting on pairs
u^{\pm}(u,\sigma)=\frac{1}{2}(|u|\sigma\pm u)
, respectively. In addition,

|u^{\pm}|=|u|\,\frac{1}{2}(1\pm e_{1}\cdot\sigma)^{1/2}
. This last identity means that
|u^{+}|=|u|\,\frac{1}{2}(1+e_{1}\cdot\sigma)^{1/2}>0
, i.e. non-vanishing, whenever
\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{d-1}:=\{e_{1}\cdot\sigma>0\}
and, analogously,
|u^{-}|=|u|\,\frac{1}{2}(1-e_{1}\cdot\sigma)^{1/2}>0
whenever
\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{-}^{d-1}:=\{e_{1}\cdot\sigma<0\}.

Thus, while the reader can observe that the definition of the constant
C_{b}(p,q)
in (
29
) may contain two singularities at the points
e_{1}\cdot\sigma=\pm 1
, (i.e in the north and south poles of the scattering sphere where
e_{1}=\hat{u}
fixed),
it becomes transparent in the next paragraph that the weak formulation associated to the bilinear collisional form
Q^{+}(f,g)(v)
, written in center of mass and relative velocity or scattering direction coordinates (
2.3
), takes a form in terms of the simpler estimates for
\mathcal{P}
from (
6.4
) described in (
29
), by associating the definition of the domain of spherical integration of the test functions to whether it is evaluated on
u^{+}(u,\sigma)
with
\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{d-1}
; or
u^{-}(u,\sigma)
with
\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{-}^{d-1}
.

Indeed, the characterization of the operator
\mathcal{P}({\it\Psi},{\it\Phi})(u):=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}{\it\Psi}(u^{+}(u,\sigma){\it\Phi}(u^{-}(u,\sigma))b(\hat{u}\cdot\sigma)d\sigma,

in the weak formulation of the
Q^{+}(f,g)(v)
is conveniently written in scattering direction coordinates (
2.3
), namely
v^{\prime}=v+u^{-}(\sigma)
and
v^{\prime}_{*}=v-u^{+}(\sigma)
.

Thus, recalling that
\tau
is the translation operator
\tau_{v}\psi(x):=\psi(x-v)
and
\mathcal{R}
the reflexion operator
\mathcal{R}\psi(x):=\psi(-x)
, then
for any given test function
\psi
set
{\it\Psi}(u^{-}(u,\sigma)):=\psi(v+u^{-}(u,\sigma))=(\tau_{-v}\psi)(u^{-}(u,\sigma))
, and

{\it\Phi}(u^{+}(u,\sigma)):=\psi(v-u^{+}(u,\sigma))=(\tau_{-v}\mathcal{R}\psi)(u^{+}(u,\sigma))

and applied the estimates from Theorem
29

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}({\it\Psi},{\it\Phi})(u)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}{\it\Psi}(u^{-}(u,\sigma))\,{\it\Phi}(u^{+}(u,\sigma))b(\hat{u}\cdot\sigma)d\sigma,

(6.7)

Thus, splitting the spherical integration domain into
\sigma\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}=\{\hat{u}\cdot\sigma<0\}\cup\{\hat{u}\cdot\sigma\geq 0\}=:\mathbb{S}_{-}^{d-1}\cup\mathbb{S}_{+}^{d-1}

is given by, the weak form of
Q^{+}(f,g)(v)
becomes

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\!Q^{+}

\displaystyle(g,f)(v)\psi(v)dv=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(v)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Phi(|u|)g(v-u)\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}_{-}^{d-1}}\psi(v+u^{-}(\sigma)){b(\hat{u}\cdot\sigma)}d\sigma\right)\!(u)dudv

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g(v_{*})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Phi(|u|)f(v_{*}+u)\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}_{+}^{d-1}}\psi(v-u^{+}(\sigma)){b(\hat{u}\cdot\sigma)}d\sigma\right)\!(u)dudv_{*}

\displaystyle=:\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\!f(v)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Phi(|u|),\tau_{-v}\mathcal{R}g(u)\left[\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b}\left((\tau_{-v}\psi)(u^{-}),1\right)\!+\!\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b}(1,(\tau_{-v}\mathcal{R}\psi)(u^{+})\right]\!(u)dudv

\displaystyle=:\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\!f(v)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Phi(|u|)\,\tau_{-v}\mathcal{R}g(u)\,\left[\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b}\left({\it\Psi}(u^{-}),1\right)\!+\!\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b}(1,{\it\Phi})(u^{+})\right]\!(u)dudv,

(6.8)

since
\it\Psi(u^{-}):=(\tau_{-v}\psi)(u^{-})
and
\it\Phi(u^{+}):=(\tau_{-v}\mathcal{R}\psi)(u^{+})
.

The potential part of the transition scattering rates

\Phi(|u|)
is the potential function defined on (
3.10
) under the assumption (
3.11
), where for the time being, we illustrate the technique for the case of
\gamma=0
, which implies
0<c_{\Phi}\leq\Phi(|u|)\leq C_{\Phi}<\infty
uniformly in
|u|
. Section 8.2 will extended the following estimate to general potential, satisfying this conditions, for any
\gamma\in(0,2]
.

Therefore, an estimate of (
6.1
) needs to evaluate the first term noting that the test function
\psi(v+u^{-}(\sigma))
acts on the first component of the
\mathcal{P}
operator (
6.4
), while the second term now the test function, not only is evaluated at a different velocity vector,
\psi(v-u^{+}(\sigma))
, but also acts on the second component of the
\mathcal{P}
.

This observation implies the constant
\tilde{C}_{b}(p,q)
can be calculated finite using the estimates (
29
) from Theorem
29
.
Therefore, since the first term of identity (
6.1
) has test function
\psi(v-u^{-}(u,\sigma))
acting on the first component of the
\mathcal{P}
from (
6.4
) while the test function in the second component
\phi
of the
\mathcal{P}
operator is taken to be unity,
hence, Theorem
29
can be applied with estimate
(
29
) by choosing
r=p=s
and
q=\infty
,
to obtain, after invoking Hölder’s inequality, finite inequality

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\tau_{-v}\mathcal{R}g)(u)\mathcal{P}^{-}_{b}({\it\Psi},1)(u)du\leq\tilde{C}_{b}(s,\infty)\|\tau_{-v}g\|_{L^{s}}\|\tau_{-v}\psi\|_{L^{s^{\prime}}}=\tilde{C}_{b}(s,\infty)\|g\|_{L^{s}}\|\psi\|_{L^{s^{\prime}}}\,,

with

\tilde{C}_{b}(s,\infty)=\int_{\mathbb{S}_{-}^{d-1}}\left(\tfrac{1-e_{1}\cdot\sigma}{2}\right)^{-\frac{d}{2s^{\prime}}}b(e_{1}\cdot\sigma)d\sigma\leq 2^{\frac{d}{2s^{\prime}}}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}_{-}^{d-1})}\,.

Analogously, the second term in (
6.1
) has the first component of
\mathcal{P}
from (
6.4
) in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{+}^{d-1})
, while the test function
\psi(v-u^{+}(\sigma))
is acting on the second component of the
\mathcal{P}
on the integrating domain
\mathbb{S}_{-}^{d-1}
. Therefore, Theorem
29
is applied with estimate (
29
), with the choice
q=r=s
and
p=\infty
, yielding

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\tau_{-v}g)(u)\mathcal{P}^{+}_{b}(1,{\it\Phi})(u)du\leq\tilde{C}_{b}(\infty,s)\|\tau_{-v}g\|_{L^{s}}\|\tau_{-v}\mathcal{R}\psi\|_{L^{s^{\prime}}}=\tilde{C}_{b}(\infty,s)\|g\|_{L^{s}}\|\psi\|_{L^{s^{\prime}}(\mathbb{S}_{+}^{d-1})}\,,

where

\tilde{C}_{b}(\infty,s)=\int_{\mathbb{S}_{+}^{d-1}}\left(\tfrac{1+e_{1}\cdot\sigma}{2}\right)^{-\frac{d}{2s^{\prime}}}b(e_{1}\cdot\sigma)d\sigma\leq 2^{\frac{d}{2s^{\prime}}}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}_{+}^{d-1})}\,.

Combining these two last estimates, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q^{+}_{b}(fig)(v)\psi(v)dv

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}_{b}(s,\infty)\|g\|_{L^{1}}\|f\|_{L^{s}}\|\psi\|_{L^{s^{\prime}}}+\tilde{C}_{b}(\infty,s)\|f\|_{L^{1}}\|g\|_{L^{s}}\|\psi\|_{L^{s^{\prime}}}

(6.9)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\frac{d}{2s}}\|\psi\|_{L^{s^{\prime}}}\Big(\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}_{-}^{d-1})}\|g\|_{L^{1}}\|f\|_{L^{s}}+\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}_{+}^{d-1})}\|f\|_{L^{1}}\|g\|_{L^{s}}\Big)\,,

or, by duality,

\|Q^{+}_{b}(f,g)\|_{L^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq 2^{\frac{d}{2s}}\Big(\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}_{-}^{d-1})}\|g\|_{L^{1}}\|f\|_{L^{s}}+\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}_{+}^{d-1})}\|f\|_{L^{1}}\|g\|_{L^{s}}\Big)\,.

(6.10)

In the quadratic case
f=g
estimate (
6.10
) reduces to

\|Q^{+}_{b}(f,f)\|_{L^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq 2^{\frac{d}{2s}}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|_{L^{1}}\|f\|_{L^{s}}\,,\qquad s\in[1,\infty]\,.

(6.11)

The expression (
6.1
) does not necessarily holds for gas mixtures with disparate masses or inelastic interacting systems since the definition of vectors
u^{\pm}:=u^{\pm}(u,\sigma)
do not enjoy the symmetry
u^{\pm}(u,-\sigma)=u^{\mp}(u,\sigma)
in such cases. Such vectors will change depending on the masses or restitution coefficients respectively. This, of course, makes the analysis a bit more subtle.

6.2.
The Carleman representation, mixing convolution, and gain of integrability

Carleman in his work
[
39
]
represents the gain part of the collisional integral with the integration in the sphere replaced by an integration along special planes. In a sense, he introduced a trick to project the sphere in
\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
in the context of the collision operator. A similar trick has been implemented in the context of radiative transfer in the non-cutoff case in
[
16
]
and has the benefit of flatten the geometry of the operator at the expense of adding weights in the integration. This representation can be quite handy in the study of fine properties of the collision operator. A modern derivation of such representation is given in
[
5
,
6
,
12
,
51
]
, which revised the Carleman representation in higher dimension ( as is given by
1
1

1

This formula correct a misprint in
[
12
]
, equation (9) and (10).
. )

Starting by setting the variables
z:=-u^{+}=\frac{1}{2}\left(|u|\sigma+u\right)
and
x:=u^{-}=\frac{1}{2}\left(|u|\sigma-u\right)
. This choices makes
to obtain
-z-u=u^{+}-\frac{1}{2}u=u^{-}
, which implies

|u|=|z+x|
.

In addition, since
|z|^{2}=\frac{1}{2}|u|^{2}(1+\hat{u}\cdot\sigma)
, then
\cos\theta={\hat{u}\cdot\hat{u^{\prime}}}=1-2\tfrac{|z|^{2}}{|u|^{2}}=1-2\tfrac{|z|^{2}}{|z+x|^{2}},
and the
d
-dimensional spherical integration element
d\sigma=|u|^{2-d}d(|u|\sigma+u)=|z+x|^{2-d}dx
,
yields the transition probability rate element in the collisional integral is written by

B(u,\hat{u}\cdot\sigma)\frac{1}{|u|^{d-2}}d\sigma=B\left(-(z+x),1-2\frac{|z|^{2}}{|z+x|^{2}}\right)\frac{1}{|z+x|^{d-2}}dx.

(6.12)

Thus, letting
g
and
h
be a pair suitable functions for which the following integral is well defined, evaluated
g(v^{\prime}_{*})=g(v-u^{+})=g(v-x)
and
h(v^{\prime})=h(v+u^{+})=h(v+z)
, the Carleman’s representation takes the form,

\begin{split}Q^{+}(g,h)(v)=\ {2^{d-1}}\!\int_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{g(v+z)}{|x|}\int_{z\cdot x=0}h(v+x)\,\!|z+x|^{2-d}\,B\Big(-(z+x),1-2\tfrac{|z|^{2}}{|z+x|^{2}}\Big)\,d\pi_{z}\,dx,\end{split}

(6.13)

where the integral element
d\pi_{z}
is the Lebesgue measure on the hyperplane
\{z:\hat{x}\cdot z=0\}.

Using the change of variables
x\to x-v
yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}&Q^{+}(g,h)(v)=2^{d-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{g(x)}{\left|v-x\right|}\int_{(v-x)\cdot z=0}\frac{\tau_{-x}h(z+(v-x))}{\left|z+(v-x)\right|^{d-2}}\,\tilde{a}\left(v,z,x\right)d\pi_{z}\,dx\\[2.0pt]
&\text{where}\qquad\tilde{a}\left(v,z,x\right):=B\left(|z+(v-x)|,1-2\tfrac{|z|^{2}}{|z+(v-x)|^{2}}\right).\end{split}

(6.14)

As noticed in
[
5
]
, applying this representation to the linear form
Q^{+}(\delta_{0},h)
one obtains the
d
-dimensional weighted Radon transform

Q^{+}(\delta_{0},h)(v)=\frac{2^{d-1}}{|v|}\int_{v\cdot z=0}\frac{h(z+v)}{|z+v|^{d-2}}\;B\left(|z+v|,1-\frac{2|z|^{2}}{|z+v|^{2}}\right)d\pi_{z}.

(6.15)

Combining (
6.14
) and (
6.15
) we obtain another representation for the collision operator, the double mixing convolution

Q^{+}(g,h)(v)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g(x)\,\tau_{x}Q^{+}(\delta_{0},\tau_{-x}h)(v)\,dx.

(6.16)

Using this representation, the
L^{2}
-norm of
Q^{+}
can be estimated in terms of the
L^{2}
-norm of
Q^{+}(\delta_{0},\cdot)
after invoking Minkowski’s integral inequality

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|Q^{+}(g,h)\big\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}&=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g(x)\tau_{x}Q^{+}(\delta_{0},\tau_{-x}h)(v)dx\right)^{2}dv\right)^{1/2}\\
&\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\tau_{x}Q^{+}(\delta_{0},\tau_{-x}h)(v)\right)^{2}dv\right)^{1/2}g(x)dx\,.\end{split}

(6.17)

Just as a minor remark, if we define the operator
\mathcal{P}_{o}(\cdot)=\mathcal{P}(\cdot,1)
, then
Q^{+}(\delta_{0},\cdot)=\mathcal{P}^{\ast}_{o}(\cdot)
the adjoint of
\mathcal{P}_{o}
.

The following proposition is a slight improvement (in terms of moments) of its analog in
[
12
]
. It shows that adding conditions on
b
, such as boundedness, one can improve the Young’s inequality of Theorem
28
. Other example of this fact can be found in
[
69
,
92
,
23
]
that deal with Sobolev regularisation.

Proposition 30

(
Gain of integrability for angular transition
b\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
).

Let
h\in L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\cap L^{\frac{2d}{2d-1}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
, with
d\geq 3
. Assume cutoff hard sphere potential
\Phi(u)=|u|\textbf{1}_{|u|\geq\varepsilon}
, with
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, and with scattering kernel
b\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
. Then,

\big\|Q^{+}_{\Phi}(\delta_{0},h)(v)\big\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq\frac{C_{d}}{\varepsilon^{\frac{d-3}{2}}}\|b\|_{\infty}\|h\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{2d-1}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,,

(6.18)

for some constant
C_{d}
depending only on the dimension.

Proof.

Using Radon’s representation (
6.15
) it follows that (for the current potential
B(u,s)=|u|\textbf{1}_{|u|\geq\varepsilon}\,b(s)
)

\displaystyle Q^{+}_{1}(\delta_{0},h)(v)

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{d-1}}{|v|}\int_{z\cdot v=0}\frac{h(z+v)}{|z+v|^{d-2}}\;B\left(|z+v|,1-\frac{2|z|^{2}}{|z+v|^{2}}\right)d\pi_{z}

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{d-1}}{|v|}\int_{z\cdot v=0}\frac{h(z+v)\textbf{1}_{|z+v|\geq\varepsilon}}{|z+v|^{d-3}}\;b\left(1-\frac{2|z|^{2}}{|z+v|^{2}}\right)d\pi_{z}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{d-1}\left\|b\right\|_{\infty}\frac{1}{|v|}\int_{z\cdot v=0}\tilde{h}(z+v)d\pi_{z}\,,

where
d\pi_{z}
is the
\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
Lebesgue’s measure and
0\leq\tilde{h}(\cdot)=h(\cdot)\textbf{1}_{|\cdot|\geq\varepsilon}/|\cdot|^{d-3}
. Using polar coordinates
v=r\sigma
, it is possible to estimate its
L^{2}
-norm in the following way

\displaystyle(2^{d-1}\left\|b\right\|_{\infty})^{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big|Q^{+}(\delta_{0},h)(v)\big|^{2}dv

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\leq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{z_{1}\cdot\sigma=0}\int_{z_{2}\cdot\sigma=0}\tilde{h}(z_{1}+r\sigma)\tilde{h}(z_{2}+r\sigma)d\pi_{z_{1}}d\pi_{z_{2}}r^{d-3}drd\sigma=:I.

Perform the change of variables, for fixed
\sigma
,
x:=z_{1}+r\sigma
. Note that
r=|x\cdot\sigma|
, therefore,

\displaystyle I=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}

\displaystyle\int_{z_{2}\cdot\sigma=0}\tilde{h}(x)\tilde{h}(z_{2}+(x\cdot\sigma)\sigma)d\pi_{z_{2}}|x\cdot\sigma|^{d-3}dxd\sigma

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{z_{2}\cdot\sigma=0}h(x)\tilde{h}(z_{2}+(x\cdot\sigma)\sigma)d\pi_{z_{2}}|\hat{x}\cdot\sigma|^{d-3}\textbf{1}_{|x|\geq\varepsilon}dxd\sigma\,.

Writing

z_{2}+(x\cdot\sigma)\sigma=x+(z_{2}+(x\cdot\sigma)\sigma-x):=x+z_{3},

it easy to check that
z_{3}\in\{z:z\cdot\sigma=0\}
. Hence,

I\leq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{z_{3}\cdot\sigma=0}h(x)\ \tilde{h}(x+z_{3})d\pi_{z_{3}}|\hat{x}\cdot\sigma|^{d-3}\textbf{1}_{|x|\geq\varepsilon}dxd\sigma.

Next, invoking the identity

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\delta_{0}(z\cdot y)\,\varphi(z)\,dz=|y|^{-1}\int_{z\cdot y=0}\varphi(z)\,d\pi_{z}\quad\iff\quad\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\delta_{0}(z\cdot\hat{y})\,\varphi(z)\,dz=\int_{z\cdot\hat{y}=0}\varphi(z)\,d\pi_{z}

valid for any smooth
\varphi
and
d\pi_{z}
is the Lebesgue measure on the hyperplane
\{z:\hat{y}\cdot z=0\}
, transforms the integration in the hyperplane

\{z_{3}\cdot\sigma=0\}
into an integration in
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, to get

\displaystyle\int_{z_{3}\cdot\sigma=0}\tilde{h}(x+z_{3})d\pi_{z_{3}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\delta(z\cdot\sigma)\tilde{h}(x+z)dz

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\delta(\hat{z}\cdot\sigma)\frac{\tilde{h}(x+z)}{|z|}dz.

Hence,

I\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{h(x)\tilde{h}(x+z)}{|z|}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\delta(\hat{z}\cdot\sigma)|\hat{x}\cdot\sigma|^{d-3}d\sigma\bigg)\textbf{1}_{|x|\geq\varepsilon}dxdz\,.

Observing that using polar coordinates with zenith
\hat{z}
it holds that

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\delta(\hat{z}\cdot\sigma)(\hat{x}\cdot\sigma)^{d-3}d\sigma\leq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\delta(\hat{z}\cdot\sigma)d\sigma=|\mathbb{S}^{d-2}|\,.

Moreover, in the region
\{|x|\geq\varepsilon\}
it follows that
\tilde{h}\leq\varepsilon^{-(d-3)}h
. Thus, one concludes that

I\leq\varepsilon^{-(d-3)}|\mathbb{S}^{d-2}|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{h(x)h(z)}{|z-x|}dzdx\,.

Invoking Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev (HLS) inequality, we have for any
r\in(1,\infty)
,

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{-(d-3)}|\mathbb{S}^{d-2}|\,\|h\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\|h\ast|x|^{-1}\|_{L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq\varepsilon^{-(d-3)}C_{d,r,p}\,\|h\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\|h\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})},

where
1/p+1/d=1+1/r
and
C_{d,r,p}
is proportional to the HLS constant. Taking the optimal choice
p=r^{\prime}
, that is
p=\frac{2d}{2d-1}
, yields
I\leq\frac{C_{d}}{\varepsilon^{d-3}}\|h\|^{2}_{L^{\frac{2d}{2d-1}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}
, which is the desired conclusion.
∎

In this way, estimate (
6.17
) and Proposition
30
readily imply that for
b\in L^{\infty}(S^{d-1})
,

\big\|Q^{+}_{\Phi}(g,h)\big\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq\tfrac{C_{d}}{\varepsilon^{\frac{d-3}{2}}}\|b\|_{\infty}\|g\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\|h\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{2d-1}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,\qquad\Phi(\cdot)=|\cdot|\textbf{1}_{|\cdot|\geq\varepsilon}\,,\;\;\varepsilon\in(0,1]\,.

(6.19)

Estimate (
6.19
) is understood as a gain of integrability for the collision operator since the
L^{2}
-norm of
Q^{+}(g,\cdot)
is estimated by the lower
L^{\frac{2d}{2d-1}}
-norm when considering a fixed integrable first entry
g
. One can use Riesz–Thorin interpolation theorem to extend this estimate to the
L^{p}
-spaces when combined with the convolution estimates of Theorem
28
, see for instance
[
75
,
12
]
.

Theorem 31

(
Gain of integrability)
.

Take functions
g,f\in(L^{1}\cap L^{p})({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
with
p\in(1,\infty)
. Let
\tilde{\Phi}(u)=\textbf{1}_{\{|u|\geq\varepsilon\}}
, with
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, and scattering kernel
b\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
. Write
b=b^{+}+b^{-}
where

b^{+}(s)=b(s)\text{1}_{\{s\geq 0\}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad b^{-}(s)=b(s)\text{1}_{\{s<0\}}\,,

are the forward and backward scattering components in
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}_{\pm}
. Then,

\displaystyle\big\|Q^{+}_{\tilde{\Phi},b^{+}}(g,h)(v)\big\|_{L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{\theta\frac{d-1}{2}}}\|b^{+}\|_{\infty}\|g\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\|h\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,,

(6.20)

\displaystyle\big\|Q^{+}_{\tilde{\Phi},b^{-}}(g,h)(v)\big\|_{L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{\theta\frac{d-1}{2}}}\|b^{-}\|_{\infty}\|h\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\|g\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,,\qquad\theta=\tfrac{2}{\max\{r,r^{\prime}\}}\,,

(6.21)

for some constant
C
depending only on the dimension
d\geq 3
. The integral exponents relate as

r>p=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccl}\frac{r}{r(1-1/d)+1/d}&\text{if}&r\in(1,2],\\[5.69046pt]
\frac{r}{2-1/d}&\text{if}&r\in[2,\infty)\,.\end{array}\right.

As a consequence,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|Q^{+}_{\tilde{\Phi},b}(g,h)(v)\big\|_{L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}&\leq\frac{C\,\|b\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon^{\theta\frac{d-1}{2}}}\Big(\|g\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\|h\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}+\|h\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\|g\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\Big)\,.\end{split}

(6.22)

Proof.

When
r\in(1,2]
one uses the end point (Theorem
28
for Maxwell interactions with
(p,q,r)=(1,1,1)
)

\big\|Q^{+}_{\tilde{\Phi},b}(g,h)\big\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|g\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\|h\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,,\qquad\tilde{\Phi}=\textbf{1}_{|\cdot|\geq\varepsilon}\,,\;\;\varepsilon\in(0,1]\,.

Interpolating with estimate (
6.19
), for any
r\in(1,2]
, one deduces that (note that
\tilde{\Phi}\leq\varepsilon^{-1}\Phi
)

\big\|Q^{+}_{\tilde{\Phi},b}(g,h)\big\|_{L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon^{\theta\frac{d-1}{2}}}\|b\|^{\theta}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|b\|^{1-\theta}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|g\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\|h\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,,\quad p=\frac{r}{r(1-1/d)+1/d}<r\,.

For the case
r\in[2,\infty)
we use the end point (Theorem
28
for Maxwell interactions with
(p,q,r)=(\infty,1,\infty)
)

\big\|Q^{+}_{\tilde{\Phi},b^{+}}(g,h)\big\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|b^{+}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|g\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,.

Note that
b^{+}
is necessary to keep the constant finite. Interpolation with (
6.19
) proves that

\big\|Q^{+}_{\tilde{\Phi},b^{+}}(g,h)\big\|_{L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon^{\theta\frac{d-1}{2}}}\|b^{+}\|^{\theta}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|b^{+}\|^{1-\theta}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|g\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\|h\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,,\quad p=\frac{r}{2-1/d}<r\,.

Here the interpolation parameter
\theta\in(0,1]
is given by

\theta=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccl}\frac{2}{r^{\prime}}&\text{if}&r\in(1,2],\\[5.69046pt]
\frac{2}{r}&\text{if}&r\in[2,\infty)\,.\end{array}\right.

This proves inequality (
6.20
) since
\|b^{+}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\leq|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|\,\|b^{+}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}
. As for inequality (
6.21
) one performs the change
\sigma\rightarrow-\sigma
to notice that

Q^{+}_{\tilde{\Phi},b^{-}}(g,h)(v)=Q^{+}_{\tilde{\Phi},\mathcal{R}b^{-}}(h,g)(v)

where
(\mathcal{R}b^{-})(s)=b^{-}(-s)
is supported in
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}_{+}
. Then one can apply inequality (
6.20
) and use that
\|\mathcal{R}b^{-}\|_{\infty}=\|b^{-}\|_{\infty}
. For the latter inequality (
6.22
) simply note that

\displaystyle\big\|Q^{+}_{\tilde{\Phi},b}(g,h)(v)\big\|_{L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}

\displaystyle=\big\|Q^{+}_{\tilde{\Phi},b^{+}}(g,h)(v)+Q^{+}_{\tilde{\Phi},b^{-}}(g,h)(v)\big\|_{L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|Q^{+}_{\tilde{\Phi},b^{+}}(g,h)(v)\big\|_{L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}+\big\|Q^{+}_{\tilde{\Phi},b^{-}}(g,h)(v)\big\|_{L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,,

and apply inequalities (
6.20
) and (
6.21
) accordingly.
∎

7.
An energy method:
L^{r}
-propagation theory

Propagation of the
L^{r}
-norms for the Boltzmann equation has been of great interest in the community for quite some time, see for instance
[
91
,
89
,
75
]
. In
[
75
]
a complete study was presented addressing not only the
L^{r}
-propagation of solutions to the space homogeneous Boltzmann equation, for
r\in(1,\infty)
, but also the propagation of Sobolev norms and convergence toward equilibrium.

In this section we use the work done in the previous section, based on
[
6
]
and
[
12
]
, to address the
L^{r}
-propagation theory filling along the way some of the issues that remained open in
[
75
]
. We free the scattering kernel
b
of any higher integrability assumption, the only requirement is
b\in L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
, and deal with the case
r=\infty
as well. In addition, we present a quite natural argument to find a lower bound for the negative collision operator to clearly address this issue with a minimal requirement on solutions. For a brief comparison between cutoff and non-cutoff
L^{r}
-propagation theories we refer the reader to
[
2
]
.

Throughout the section we use the
L^{p}_{\ell}
spaces which, we recall, are defined as:

L^{p}_{\ell}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\ =\ \Big\{\ g\;\text{measurable}\ :\ \|g\|_{L^{p}_{\ell}}\ =\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}g\|_{L^{p}}\ <\ \infty\Big\}\,,\qquad p\geq 1,\quad\ell\in\mathbb{R}\,.

(7.1)

7.1.
Estimating the Dirichlet form of the collision operator for hard potentials

This subsection focuses on estimates of following from Theorem
28
and Theorem
31
in order to find a suitable estimate for the collision Dirichlet form

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q^{+}(f,f)(v)\,f(v)^{r-1}\langle v\rangle^{r\,\ell}dv\,,\qquad\ell\geq 0,\quad r\in(1,\infty)\,,

(7.2)

with a transition probability
B(|u|,\hat{u}\cdot\sigma)=\Phi(|u|)b(\hat{u}\cdot\sigma)
with the potential function
\Phi
satisfying conditions from (
3.10
) and
b\in L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
. Such estimates are central when implementing an energy method for the Boltzmann equation. As stated earlier, these ideas started in
[
89
,
91
]
and were successfully concluded in
[
75
]
. They are inspired in older ideas used for the analysis of the linearized Boltzmann equation where the gain operator is broken down in a big compact (smoothing) part that satisfies a gain of integrability property and a small rough part. Then, one uses the loss part of the collision operator to control such small rough part. In the following argument, we use Theorem
31
which reduces considerably the technicalities of the original arguments given in
[
75
]
.

The main technicality of this new argument is to handle the general case
b\in L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
. To this end, one has to make a proper decomposition of the integration domains by writting the collisional kernel as the sum of nonnegative functions as follows

|\cdot|^{\gamma}=\Phi(\cdot)=\Phi_{l}(\cdot)+\Phi_{s}(\cdot)\,,\qquad b(\cdot)=b^{\infty}(\cdot)+b^{1}(\cdot)\in L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})\,.

(7.3)

That is, the component
b^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})\in L^{\infty}
is a good approximation for
b\in L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
and
\Phi_{l}(u)
relate to large relative velocities of the potential by choosing sufficiently large positive parameters
R
and
\tilde{a}
to make

\displaystyle\Phi_{l}(u)=\Phi(u)\,\text{1}_{\{|u|\geq 1/R\}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|b^{1}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\leq\tilde{a}^{-1}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\,,

(7.4)

in order to estimate
the gain part of collision operator with a collision cross section
|u|^{\gamma}\,b(\hat{u}\cdot\sigma)
as a sum of terms

Q^{+}_{\Phi,b}(f,f)=Q^{+}_{\Phi_{l},b^{\infty}}(f,f)+Q^{+}_{\Phi,b^{1}}(f,f)+Q^{+}_{\Phi_{s},b^{\infty}}(f,f)\,.

(7.5)

Before estimating each term in the right of (
7.5
), recall center of mass and relative velocities coordinates introduced in (
2.3
) to be used in the form

u^{+}=\frac{u+|u|\sigma}{2}=v-v^{\prime}_{*}\qquad\text{and}\qquad u^{-}=\frac{u-|u|\sigma}{2}=v^{\prime}-v\,,

as defined in (
2.3
). Then the following inequalities hold

\displaystyle{\bf i)}\ \text{If}\ \ \widehat{u}\cdot\sigma\geq 0,\ \text{then}\ |u|=|u^{+}|\sqrt{\tfrac{2}{1+\widehat{u}\cdot\sigma}}=|v-v^{\prime}_{*}|\sqrt{\tfrac{2}{1+\widehat{u}\cdot\sigma}}\leq\langle v^{\prime}_{*}\rangle\langle v\rangle\sqrt{\tfrac{2}{1+\widehat{u}\cdot\sigma}}\leq\sqrt{2}\,\langle v^{\prime}_{*}\rangle\,\langle v\rangle\,,

and

(7.6)

\displaystyle{\bf ii)}\ \text{If}\ \ \widehat{u}\cdot\sigma\leq 0,\ \text{then}\ |u|=|u^{-}|\sqrt{\tfrac{2}{1-\widehat{u}\cdot\sigma}}=|v-v^{\prime}|\sqrt{\tfrac{2}{1-\widehat{u}\cdot\sigma}}\leq\langle v^{\prime}\rangle\langle v\rangle\sqrt{\tfrac{2}{1-\widehat{u}\cdot\sigma}}\leq\sqrt{2}\,\langle v^{\prime}\rangle\,\langle v\rangle\,,\

Thus, from (
7.1
) it follows for any
r\in(1,\infty)
and
\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}=1

\displaystyle\begin{split}|u|^{\gamma}&=|u^{\prime}|^{\frac{\gamma}{r}}|u|^{\frac{\gamma}{r^{\prime}}}\leq 2^{\frac{\gamma}{2r^{\prime}}}\,\langle v^{\prime}_{*}\rangle^{\gamma}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{r}}\,\langle v\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{r^{\prime}}}\quad\text{ for the case {\bf i)}}\,,\\
|u|^{\gamma}&=|u^{\prime}|^{\frac{\gamma}{r}}|u|^{\frac{\gamma}{r^{\prime}}}\leq 2^{\frac{\gamma}{2r^{\prime}}}\,\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{\gamma}\langle v^{\prime}_{*}\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{r}}\,\langle v\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{r^{\prime}}}\quad\text{ for the case {\bf ii)}}\,.\end{split}

(7.7)

Further, write
b^{\infty}=b^{\infty}_{+}+b^{\infty}_{-}
the forward and backward scattering components supported in
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}_{\pm}
respectively, then from (
7.7
) it holds that

\displaystyle Q^{+}_{\Phi_{l},b^{\infty}_{+}}(f,f)(v)\leq 2^{\frac{\gamma}{2r^{\prime}}}\,\langle v\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{r^{\prime}}}\,Q^{+}_{\tilde{\Phi},b^{\infty}_{+}}(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\gamma}\,f,\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{r}}\,f)(v)

\displaystyle Q^{+}_{\Phi_{l},b^{\infty}_{-}}(f,f)(v)\leq 2^{\frac{\gamma}{2r^{\prime}}}\,\langle v\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{r^{\prime}}}\,Q^{+}_{\tilde{\Phi},b^{\infty}_{-}}(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{r}}\,f,\langle\cdot\rangle^{\gamma}\,f)(v)

(7.8)

Thus, the corresponding Dirichlet form for the first term of (
7.5
) is estimated as follows: using Theorem
31
inequality (
6.20
) and standard Lebesgue’s interpolation one has

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q^{+}_{\Phi_{l},b^{\infty}_{+}}(f,f)(v)

\displaystyle f(v)^{r-1}dv\leq 2^{\frac{\gamma}{2r^{\prime}}}\|Q^{+}_{\tilde{\Phi},b^{\infty}_{+}}\big(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\gamma}\,f,\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{r}}f\big)\|_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{r-1}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(7.9)

\displaystyle\leq C_{R,b^{\infty}_{+}}\|f\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|_{L^{p}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{r-1}_{L^{r}_{{\frac{\gamma}{r}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{R,b^{\infty}_{+}}\|f\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{1-\theta_{r}}_{L^{1}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{r+\theta_{r}-1}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,,\qquad\theta_{r}\in(0,1)\,.

Similarly, Theorem
31
inequality (
6.21
) renders

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q^{+}_{\Phi_{l},b^{\infty}_{-}}(f,f)(v)

\displaystyle f(v)^{r-1}dv\leq 2^{\frac{\gamma}{2r^{\prime}}}\|Q^{+}_{\tilde{\Phi},b^{\infty}_{-}}\big(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{r}}\,f,\langle\cdot\rangle^{\gamma}f\big)\|_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{r-1}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(7.10)

\displaystyle\leq C_{R,b^{\infty}_{-}}\|f\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|_{L^{p}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{r-1}_{L^{r}_{{\frac{\gamma}{r}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{R,b^{\infty}_{-}}\|f\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{1-\theta_{r}}_{L^{1}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{r+\theta_{r}-1}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,,\qquad\theta_{r}\in(0,1)\,.

Here the constants
C_{R,b^{\infty}_{\pm}}=C\,R^{\frac{d-1}{\max\{r,r^{\prime}\}}}\;\|b^{\infty}_{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}}
blows up as
R
and
\tilde{a}
increase. Writing

Q^{+}_{\Phi_{l},b^{\infty}}(f,f)(v)=Q^{+}_{\Phi_{l},b^{\infty}_{+}}(f,f)(v)+Q^{+}_{\Phi_{l},b^{\infty}_{-}}(f,f)(v)\,,

one uses estimates (
7.9
) and (
7.10
) to conclude that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q^{+}_{\Phi_{l},b^{\infty}}(f,f)(v)\,f(v)^{r-1}dv\leq C_{R,b^{\infty}}\|f\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{1-\theta_{r}}_{L^{1}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{r+\theta_{r}-1}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,,\qquad\theta_{r}\in(0,1)\,.

(7.11)

The second term in (
7.5
) is estimated in a similar manner using Theorem
28
instead of Theorem
31
. Writing, as before,
b^{1}=b^{1}_{+}+b^{1}_{-}
the forward and backward scattering components supported in
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}_{\pm}
respectively, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q^{+}_{\Phi,b^{1}_{+}}(f,f)(v)f(v)^{r-1}dv

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\frac{\gamma}{2r^{\prime}}}\|Q^{+}_{b^{1}_{+}}\big(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\gamma}f,\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{r}}f\big)\|_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{r-1}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(7.12)

\displaystyle\leq C\|b^{1}_{+}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\,\|f\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\tilde{a}^{-1}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q^{+}_{\Phi,b^{1}_{-}}(f,f)(v)f(v)^{r-1}dv

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\frac{\gamma}{2r^{\prime}}}\|Q^{+}_{b^{1}_{-}}\big(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{r}}f,\langle\cdot\rangle^{\gamma}f\big)\|_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{r-1}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(7.13)

\displaystyle\leq C\|b^{1}_{-}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\,\|f\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\tilde{a}^{-1}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,.

Consequently, from inequalities (
7.12
) and (
7.13
) and since

Q^{+}_{\Phi,b^{1}}(f,f)(v)=Q^{+}_{\Phi,b^{1}_{+}}(f,f)(v)+Q^{+}_{\Phi,b^{1}_{-}}(f,f)(v)\,,

it holds that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q^{+}_{\Phi,b^{1}}(f,f)(v)f(v)^{r-1}dv\leq C\,\tilde{a}^{-1}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,.

(7.14)

For the latter term in (
7.5
) simply note, since
\Phi_{s}(u)=|u|^{\gamma}\text{1}_{\{|u|<1/R\}}\leq R^{-\gamma}
, that

Q^{+}_{\Phi_{s},b^{\infty}}(f,f)\leq R^{-\gamma}Q^{+}_{b^{\infty}}(f,f)\,.

Therefore, by exploiting the quadratic nature of the estimate, Theorem
28
used in
b^{\infty}_{\pm}
readily gives

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q^{+}_{\Phi_{s},b^{\infty}}(f,f)(v)f(v)^{r-1}dv\leq C\,R^{-\gamma}\,\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\,\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,.

(7.15)

Gathering estimates (
7.11
), (
7.14
), and (
7.15
), one has

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q^{+}(f,f)(v)f(v)^{r-1}dv\leq C\|f\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big(\big(\tilde{a}^{-1}+R^{-\gamma}\big)

\displaystyle\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle+C_{R,b^{\infty}}\|f\|^{1-\theta_{r}}_{L^{1}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{r+\theta_{r}-1}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big)\,.

Adding the weight
\langle\cdot\rangle^{r\,\ell}
in the Dirichlet form is not a problem after using the inequality

\langle v\rangle^{r\,\ell}=\langle v\rangle^{\ell}\langle v\rangle^{(r-1)\,\ell}\leq\langle v^{\prime}_{*}\rangle^{\ell}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{\ell}\langle v\rangle^{(r-1)\,\ell}\,.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}

\displaystyle Q^{+}(f,f)(v)f(v)^{r-1}\langle v\rangle^{r\ell}dv\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q^{+}\big(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}f,\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}f\big)(v)\big(f(v)\langle v\rangle^{\ell}\big)^{r-1}dv

\displaystyle\leq C\|f\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma+\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big(\big(\tilde{a}^{-1}+R^{-\gamma}\big)\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}+\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+C_{R,b^{\infty}}\|f\|^{1-\theta_{r}}_{L^{1}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}+\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{r+\theta_{r}-1}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}+\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big)\,.

Furthermore, if
f
satisfies the condition of Lemma
8
one has that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q^{-}(f,f)(v)\langle v\rangle^{r\,\ell}f(v)^{r-1}dv

\displaystyle=\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big(f(v)\langle v\rangle^{k}\big)^{r}\big(f\ast|\cdot|^{\gamma}\big)(v)dv

\displaystyle\geq c_{lb}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big(f(v)\langle v\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{r}+\ell}\big)^{r}dv=c_{lb}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\ell}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,.

As a consequence, adding these latter two estimates we obtain, for any
R>0
,
\tilde{a}>0
and
r\in(1,\infty)
, that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q(f,f)(v)\langle v\rangle^{r\,\ell}f(v)^{r-1}dv&\leq C\,\|f\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma+\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big(\big(\tilde{a}^{-1}+R^{-\gamma}\big)\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}+\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&\qquad+C_{R,b^{\infty}}\|f\|^{1-\theta_{r}}_{L^{1}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}+\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{r+\theta_{r}-1}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}+\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big)-c_{lb}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\ell}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,.\end{split}

(7.16)

Before entering in the specifics of the statement of the following theorem, based on previous discussion, we mention the constant

C_{\ell,\alpha}:=\sup_{t\geq 0}\|f(t)\|_{L^{1}_{\alpha+\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}(t)=\sup_{t\geq 0}m_{\frac{\alpha+\ell}{2}}(t)\leq\max\Big\{m_{\frac{\alpha+\ell}{2}}[f_{0}],\,\mathbb{E}_{\frac{\alpha+\ell}{2}}\Big\}<\infty\,,\quad\alpha\geq 0\,,\quad\ell\geq 0\,,

(7.17)

with the
(\alpha+\ell)/2
-moments globally controlled by estimates (
16
) and (
17
) developed in Theorem
17
.

Theorem 32

(Hard potentials)
.

Let
b\in L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
,
\gamma\in(0,1]
,
\ell\geq 0
, and
r\in(1,\infty)
. Assume that
f
satisfies the hypothesis of the Lower Bound Lemma
8
and
m_{\frac{\gamma+\ell}{2}}[f_{0}]<\infty
. Then, there exists a constant
\bar{C}_{\gamma}:=\bar{C}_{\gamma}(\|b\|_{1},C_{\ell,\gamma})
, estimated below in (
7.19
) and (
7.20
), such that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q(f,f)(v)\langle v\rangle^{r\,\ell}f(v)^{r-1}dv

\displaystyle\leq\bar{C}_{\gamma}-\tfrac{c_{lb}}{2}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\ell}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,,\qquad r>1,

(7.18)

with
C_{\ell,\gamma}
defined in (
7.17
) controlling the propagation of moments estimates (
16
)and (
17
) developed in Theorem
17
and
c_{lb}
was given in Lemma
8
.

Proof.

Using the hypothesis, estimate (
7.16
) reduces to

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q(f,f)(v)\langle v\rangle^{r\,\ell}f(v)^{r-1}dv

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ell,\gamma}\Big(\big(\tilde{a}^{-1}+R^{-\gamma}\big)\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}+\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\qquad+C_{R,b^{\infty}}\,C^{1-\theta_{r}}_{\ell,\gamma}\|f\|^{r+\theta_{r}-1}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}+\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big)-c_{lb}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\ell}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{R,b^{\infty}}\,C^{1-\theta_{r}}_{\ell,\gamma}\|f\|^{r+\theta_{r}-1}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}+\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}-\tfrac{3c_{lb}}{4}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\ell}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,,

where, in the last inequality, the parameters
R
and
\tilde{a}
are chosen sufficiently large so that
C_{\ell,\gamma}\big(\tilde{a}^{-1}+R^{-\gamma}\big)\leq\frac{c_{lb}}{4}
. For instance, we can choose

\tilde{a}=\frac{8\,C_{\ell,\gamma}}{c_{lb}}\,,\quad\text{and then}\quad R=\tilde{a}^{1/\gamma}=\Big(\frac{8\,C_{\ell,\gamma}}{c_{lb}}\Big)^{1/\gamma}\,.

(7.19)

Furthermore, since
\theta_{r}\in(0,1)
, we can use Young’s inequality controlling

C_{R,b^{\infty}}\,C^{2-\theta_{r}}_{\ell,\gamma}\|f\|^{r+\theta_{r}-1}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}+\ell}}\leq\Big(\frac{4}{c_{lb}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}}\Big)^{\frac{r+\theta_{r}-1}{1-\theta_{r}}}\big(C_{R,b^{\infty}}\,C^{2-\theta_{r}}_{\ell,\gamma}\big)^{\frac{r}{1-\theta_{r}}}+\tfrac{c_{lb}}{4}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\ell}},

to obtain that

C_{R,b^{\infty}}\,C^{2-\theta_{r}}_{\ell,\gamma}\|f\|^{r+\theta_{r}-1}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{r}+\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}-\tfrac{3c_{lb}}{4}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\ell}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\\
\leq\Big(\frac{4}{c_{lb}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}}\Big)^{\frac{r+\theta_{r}-1}{1-\theta_{r}}}\big(C_{R,b^{\infty}}\,C^{2-\theta_{r}}_{\ell,\gamma}\big)^{\frac{r}{1-\theta_{r}}}-\tfrac{c_{lb}}{4}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}_{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\ell}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,.

Recalling that
C_{R,b^{\infty}}=C\,R^{\frac{d-1}{\max\{r,r^{\prime}\}}}\;\|b^{\infty}\|_{L^{\infty}}
, we can rewrite the first term in the right hand side as

\bar{C}_{\gamma}=\bar{C}_{\gamma}(b,r,C_{\ell,\gamma}):=\|b\|^{1-\frac{r}{1-\theta_{r}}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|b^{\infty}\|^{\frac{r}{1-\theta_{r}}}_{L^{\infty}}\Big(\frac{4}{c_{lb}}\Big)^{\frac{r+\theta_{r}-1}{1-\theta_{r}}}\Big(C\,R^{\frac{d-1}{\max\{r,r^{\prime}\}}}\,C^{2-\theta_{r}}_{\ell,\gamma}\Big)^{\frac{r}{1-\theta_{r}}}\,,

(7.20)

which proves the result.
∎

7.1.1.
The Maxwell molecules case

Note that in order to control
Q^{+}_{\Phi_{s},b^{\infty}}
, the small relative velocities, the kinetic potential
\Phi(u)
needs to vanish as
|u|\rightarrow 0
. This is not the case for the Maxwell Molecules case. Indeed, Maxwell molecules is a critical case for uniform propagation of
L^{r}
-integrability for general initial data in
L^{r}
in the sense that as soon as
\gamma<0
, i.e. soft potential case, the uniform propagation of moments is lost for general initial data as discussed in
[
37
]
let alone the
L^{r}_{\ell}
-norms
2
2

2

For the non-cutoff Boltzmann problem this is not the case and the analysis is quite different.
. In order to compensate for this criticality issue, we will use the additional compactness gained through propagation of entropy after noticing that we implicitly have the additional requirement on the initial data

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f_{0}(v)\ln\big(f_{0}(v)\big)dv<\infty\,.

In our context
f_{0}\in\big(L^{1}_{2}\cap L^{r}\big)(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for
r>1
. As a consequence, the initial entropy is finite since for any
1<r<\frac{d}{d-1}\leq 2

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}

\displaystyle f_{0}(v)\ln\big(f_{0}(v)\big)dv\bigg|\leq\int_{\{f_{0}\leq 1\}}\big|f_{0}(v)\ln\big(f_{0}(v)\big)\big|dv+\int_{\{f_{0}\geq 1\}}|f_{0}(v)\ln\big(f_{0}(v)\big)|dv

\displaystyle\leq C_{r}\int_{\{f_{0}\leq 1\}}\big|f_{0}(v)\big|^{\frac{1}{r}}dv+\int_{\{f_{0}\geq 1\}}|f_{0}(v)|^{r}dv

\displaystyle\leq C_{r,d}\bigg(\int_{\{f_{0}\leq 1\}}f_{0}(v)\langle v\rangle^{r}dv\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r}}+\int_{\{f_{0}\geq 1\}}|f_{0}(v)|^{r}dv=:C\Big(d,\|f_{0}\|_{(L^{1}_{2}\cap L^{r})(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big)<+\infty.

In the second estimate we used that
|x\ln(x)|\leq C_{p}\,x^{\frac{1}{r}}
, for any
r>1
and
x\in[0,1]
. Furthermore, it is well known that using energy conservation and entropy propagation it follows for the solution of the homogeneous Boltzmann equation
f(v,t)
, see
[
47
, page 329]
or more recently
[
13
, Lemma A.1]
, that

\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(v,t)\big|\ln\big(f(v,t)\big)\big|dv\leq C\bigg(\int f_{0}\ln(f_{0})dv,\int f_{0}|v|^{2}dv\bigg)=:C_{\mathcal{M}}\,.

(7.21)

Theorem 33

(Maxwell Molecules)
.

Let
b\in L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
. Fix
\ell\geq 0
and assume that

\|f\|_{L^{1}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=C_{\ell,0}<\infty\,,\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(v)\big|\ln\big(f(v)\big)\big|dv=C_{\mathcal{M}}<\infty\,.

Then, there exists a constant
\bar{C}_{0}:=\bar{C}_{0}(\|b\|_{1},C_{\ell,0},C_{\mathcal{M}})
, estimated below in (
7.23
),(
7.26
) and (
7.27
), such that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q_{0}(f,f)(v)\langle v\rangle^{r\,\ell}f(v)^{r-1}dv

\displaystyle\leq\bar{C}_{0}-\tfrac{1}{2}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}_{\ell}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,,\quad\forall\,r\in[1,\infty)\,.

(7.22)

Proof.

Use the decomposition
b(s)=b(s)\text{1}_{\{|s|<1-\varepsilon\}}+b(s)\text{1}_{\{|s|\geq 1-\varepsilon\}}=:b_{1}^{\varepsilon}+b_{2}^{\varepsilon}
. Since
\|b_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}
decreases monotonically to zero with
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, we can choose
\varepsilon:=\varepsilon(R)
sufficiently small such that

\|b_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\leq R^{-1}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\,,\qquad R>0\,.

(7.23)

Write
Q^{+}_{0,b}(f,f)=Q^{+}_{0,b^{\varepsilon}_{1}}(f,f)+Q^{+}_{0,b^{\varepsilon}_{2}}(f,f)
.

On the one hand invoking Theorem
28
, used in
b^{\varepsilon}_{2,\pm}
the upper and lower scattering of
b^{\varepsilon}_{2}
exploiting the quadratic nature of the estimate, and the fact that
\langle v\rangle\leq\langle v^{\prime}_{*}\rangle\langle v^{\prime}\rangle
we find that

\displaystyle\|Q^{+}_{0,b^{\varepsilon}_{2}}(f,f)\|_{L^{r}_{k}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\|Q^{+}_{0,b^{\varepsilon}_{2}}(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}f,\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}f)\|_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq 2^{\frac{d}{2r^{\prime}}+1}\|b^{\varepsilon}_{2}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|_{L^{1}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|_{L^{r}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\frac{d}{2r^{\prime}}+1}R^{-1}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|_{L^{1}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|_{L^{r}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,.

On the other hand, the inequality
\langle v\rangle\leq\langle v^{\prime}_{*}\rangle+\langle v^{\prime}\rangle
lead us to

Q^{+}_{0,b^{\varepsilon}_{1}}(f,f)\,\langle v\rangle^{\ell}\leq 2^{\ell-1}Q^{+}_{0,b^{\varepsilon}_{1}}(f,\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}f)+2^{k-1}Q^{+}_{0,b^{\varepsilon}_{1}}(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}f,f)\,.

Let us start with the first term in the right. Note that for
K>1

\displaystyle Q^{+}_{0,b^{\varepsilon}_{1}}(f,\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}f)

\displaystyle=Q^{+}_{0,b^{\varepsilon}_{1}}(f\,\text{1}_{\{f\leq K\}},\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}f)+Q^{+}_{0,b^{\varepsilon}_{1}}(f\,\text{1}_{\{f>K\}},\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}f)

\displaystyle\leq Q^{+}_{0,b^{\varepsilon}_{1}}(f\,\text{1}_{\{f\leq K\}},\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}f)+\ln(K)^{-1}\,Q^{+}_{0,b^{\varepsilon}_{1}}(f\,\ln[f],\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}f)\,.

Using again Theorem
28
, one concludes that

\displaystyle\|Q^{+}_{0,b^{\varepsilon}_{1}}(f\,\ln[f],\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}f)\|_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{d}{2r^{\prime}}}\,2^{\frac{d}{2r^{\prime}}}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\,\ln[f]\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|_{L^{r}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,,

\displaystyle\text{and}\qquad\|Q^{+}_{0,b^{\varepsilon}_{1}}(f\,\text{1}_{\{f\leq K\}},\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}f)\|_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{d}{2r^{\prime}}}\,2^{\frac{d}{2r^{\prime}}}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\,\text{1}_{\{f\leq K\}}\|_{L^{\frac{2r}{r+1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}f\|_{L^{\frac{2r}{r+1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{d}{2r^{\prime}}}\,K^{\frac{1}{2r^{\prime}}}\,2^{\frac{d}{2r^{\prime}}}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|^{\frac{r+1}{2r}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|_{L^{\frac{2r}{r+1}}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,.

Use, in the latter inequality, the interpolation

\|f\|_{L^{\frac{2r}{r+1}}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq\|f\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{r}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,,

and gather the estimates to conclude that

\displaystyle\|Q^{+}_{0,b^{\varepsilon}_{1}}(f,\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}f)\|_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{d}{2r^{\prime}}}\,2^{\frac{d}{2r^{\prime}}}\,\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\Big(\ln(K)^{-1}

\displaystyle\|f\ln[f]\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|_{L^{r}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle+K^{\frac{1}{2r^{\prime}}}\,\|f\|^{1+\frac{1}{2r}}_{L^{1}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{r}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big)\,.

Since
Q^{+}_{0,b^{\varepsilon}_{1}}(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}f,f)=Q^{+}_{0,\mathcal{R}b^{\varepsilon}_{1}}(f,\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}f)
an identical estimate follows for the second term

\displaystyle Q^{+}_{0,b^{\varepsilon}_{1}}(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}f,f)\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{d}{2r^{\prime}}}\,2^{\frac{d}{2r^{\prime}}}\,\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\Big(\ln(K)^{-1}\|f\ln[f]\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{r}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle+\,K^{\frac{1}{2r^{\prime}}}\,\|f\|^{1+\frac{1}{2r}}_{L^{1}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{r}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big)\,.

Overall, we are led to the estimate

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|Q^{+}_{0,b}(f,f)\|_{L^{r}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}&\leq 2^{\frac{d}{2r^{\prime}}}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\Big(\ln(K)^{-1}2^{\ell-1}\varepsilon^{-\frac{d}{2r^{\prime}}}\|f\ln[f]\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|_{L^{r}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&\qquad+2^{\ell-1}\varepsilon^{-\frac{d}{2r^{\prime}}}\,K^{\frac{1}{2r^{\prime}}}\,\|f\|^{1+\frac{1}{2r}}_{L^{1}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{r}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+R^{-1}\|f\|_{L^{1}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|_{L^{r}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big)\\
&=2^{\frac{d}{2r^{\prime}}}\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\Big(2^{\ell-1}\varepsilon^{-\frac{d}{2r^{\prime}}}\,K^{\frac{1}{2r^{\prime}}}\,C^{1+\frac{1}{2r}}_{\ell}\|f\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{r}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&\hskip 71.13188pt+\big(2^{\ell-1}\varepsilon^{-\frac{d}{2r^{\prime}}}\,C_{\mathcal{M}}\,\ln(K)^{-1}+C_{\ell,0}R^{-1}\big)\|f\|_{L^{r}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big)\,.\end{split}

(7.24)

With this estimate at hand, the upper control goes as follows. Using Hölder’s inequality one notices that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q_{0}(f,f)(v)\langle v\rangle^{r\,\ell}&f^{r-1}(v)dv\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q^{+}_{0}(f,f)(v)\langle v\rangle^{\ell}\big(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\ell}f\big)^{r-1}(v)dv-\|b\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})}\|f\|^{r}_{L^{r}_{\ell}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
